# Delivery register: intents I01 to I07 (dispatched 2026-10-18)

This is the durable decision and continuation register for the owner-authorized delivery of roadmap intents #283 to #289. It is updated at every checkpoint. The requirements live in [#278](https://github.com/Jacob-J-Thomas/GraphGoblin/issues/278) and the canonical issue bodies; this file records how they are being delivered, what was decided along the way, and how to continue after an interruption.

## 1. Authority and scope

- **Authority:** owner instruction of 2026-10-18 to the orchestrating Fable session: deliver intents I01 to I07 with their approved, nondeferred UoWs and bolts; own the complete intent outcomes. Recorded in [#279](https://github.com/Jacob-J-Thomas/GraphGoblin/issues/279) (comment of 2026-10-18).
- **Baseline:** `main` at `cfae6966029fe181c2064637c6ca8b2dfc31da37`; no open PRs; PRs #338, #339, #340, #342 merged; Claude delivery issues closed.
- **Released:** 7 intents, 21 UoWs, 59 bolts (table in the #279 comment). Reconciled against live GitHub on 2026-10-18: no scope drift.
- **Held:** I08 #290, I09 #291, deferred #262, #345, #335, #344, later intents #326 to #334.
- **Protected (never modified):** #38, #97, #98, #118, #152, #164, #165, #166, #168, #169, #171, #173, #341; PRs #275, #277, #338, #339, #340, #342.
- **Gates:** #280 satisfied (owner comment 2026-10-18, SHA `dcd90e6`). #281 evidence recorded 2026-10-18 (#169/#171/#166 closed, #341 accepted, baseline `cfae696`). #282 readiness recorded 2026-10-18 (Codex CLI 0.160.0 logged in via ChatGPT; Claude Code 2.1.292 logged in via claude.ai; no credentials exported).
- **Current authority and merge gate (owner steering 2026-10-25, D-042):** the orchestrator implements, integrates and merges all remaining in-scope work when its technical gates pass, without per-PR owner permission, including PRs #355 and #375. The gate is an independent auditor's explicit `SIGN-OFF` at the exact head and target (D-029), bounded by D-035's three-scan cap, which is never reset; at the cap the documented completion path applies. The owner's checkout, app and store are not reset, deleted, upgraded or restarted. Human QA follows the merge and is recorded separately. The single-session gate in the next bullet was replaced by D-029 on 2026-10-22 and by D-042; it stays as the record of the 2026-10-21 rule.
- **Acceptance and merge authority (corrected 2026-10-21; D-024, D-025):** approved, objectively verifiable behaviour is accepted through independent agent review, tests appropriate to the acceptance criteria and independent QA. The owner never requested blanket human acceptance of such behaviour before merge: the "owner acceptance" and "owner product review" gate recorded in this file from 2026-10-18 to 2026-10-20 (D-001, D-009, the section 4 rows and the section 9 requests) was an inference from this repository's own docs/15 label wording, which the Codex coordination had presented as an owner requirement. The inference is corrected here, not reversed by the owner; the historical rows stand as written. **Final merge gate:** from 2026-10-21 no I01 to I07 PR merges, enters a merge queue or gets auto-merge until the designated Codex audit session `01a156ac-2e81-7d22-bce7-e6fe79719a41` has explicitly signed off that PR at its exact head for its stated target or integration context (recorded in the #279 merge-gate section). New commits, conflict resolutions, rebases, restacks or retargeting need renewed sign-off; silence is not approval; approval does not transfer between PRs; no open PR is signed off at the time of writing. Implementation, fixes, tests, conflict resolution, independent review, QA and opening or advancing PRs continue. `human-in-the-loop` now means human QA of the merged build for feature work with substantial ambiguity or behaviour agents cannot readily verify, tracked separately from merge (section 4b); it is not a pre-merge review and does not apply to UI work as such.

## 2. Routing

Owner routing of 2026-10-18 supersedes older label assignments. Opus 5.5 implements complicated and visual work and reviews Sol; GPT-6.1 Sol implements most bounded work and reviews Opus; Luna does heartbeat monitoring only; Astra is escalation. All substantive work at xhigh. Implementer, reviewer and QA are separate; cross-vendor review is mandatory. Labels updated accordingly (see #279 comment).

| Implementer       | Bolts                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ----------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Opus (review Sol) | #266, #188, #247, #242, #251, #224, #319, #343, #33, #205, #259, #256, #129, #255, #116, #186, #240, #243, #320, #321, #185, #187, #322, #268                                                                   |
| Sol (review Opus) | #263, #127, #67, #241, #316, #317, #264, #274, #114, #250, #223, #318, #230, #234, #272, #226, #257, #225, #227, #233, #140, #246, #232, #190, #117, #179, #204, #206, #270, #244, #265, #102, #245, #231, #236 |

Live-provider verification (bounded real turns through the product's harness) is done by a Claude worker or the orchestrator, because the Codex sandbox cannot write to the Claude CLI's home directory. Codex QA covers everything else.

## 3. Dependency schedule

Native blocked-by graph (bolts only; #279/#280/#281/#282 gates are satisfied or recorded):

- Wave A (no bolt prerequisite): #263, #316, #274, #250, #223, #230, #234, #266, #247, #272, #225, #227, #233, #140, #246, #232, #117, #179, #204, #206, #243, #102, #231, #236, #129.
- Wave B: #127, #67, #114, #190 after #263; #317 after #316; #318 after #223; #188 after #266; #226, #257 after #272; #242 after #247 and #272; #343, #244, #116, #245 after #247; #205 after #204; #270 after #243; #185 after #246; #240 after #231.
- Wave C: #241 after #67; #264 after #316 and #241; #186, #259, #268 after #190; #251, #320 after #242; #224 after #226 and #242; #33 after #244 and #247; #255 after #116 and #245.
- Wave D: #319 after #224; #321 after #251; #187 after #320; #265 after #33 and #251; #256 after #251, #320, #321, #116.
- Wave E: #322 after #187.

Concurrency cap: about eight workers at once (CPU and test-pool contention). Hot files with a single owner per wave: `packages/engine/src/run-manager.ts`, `apps/api/src/routes/runs.ts`, `apps/mcp/src/tools.ts`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.

Rows written before 2026-10-21 say "merge only after owner acceptance", "owner QA request posted" or "waiting on the owner": those words record the gate as it was then understood (D-009, superseded by D-024) and are kept as history. The current acceptance state of every package is in section 4b.

### 4b. Acceptance state per package (from 2026-10-21)

Each column is a separate recorded state and none substitutes for another. "Sign-off" is the designated Codex session's explicit approval of the PR at the named head and target (#279 merge-gate section; pending until granted, with the evidence link recorded here when it is). "Human QA" is post-merge human QA under the corrected `human-in-the-loop` definition: not required, pending merge, pending human QA, or completed with an evidence link, with the recorded rationale. Packages merged before 2026-10-21 landed under the authority then in force; the gate did not exist and is recorded as not applicable for them.

## 6. Engineering parameters chosen

| Parameter               | Value                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Package                                                                                                                                   | Why                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Impact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| HTTP probe body cap     | 65,536 bytes, enforced before decoding and parsing; constant in `packages/engine/src/ports.ts` as part of the `HttpProbePort` contract; typed failure `HTTP_PROBE_BODY_OVERFLOW`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | WP-D3 (#117)                                                                                                                              | Matches the existing items-mode poll budget and the heartbeat display slice; the engine owns the HTTP-shaped port contracts and both consumers (poll in the API, heartbeat in the engine) rely on the guarantee | Single-result HTTP polls and heartbeat probes whose JSON exceeds 64 KiB previously parsed the full body and only sliced it for display; they now fail explicitly. A GitHub REST list page with 30 to 100 issues exceeds the cap. The CHANGELOG upgrade note names how to find affected loops and the script-probe alternative. Named in the PR for owner visibility; a per-request `maxBodyBytes` is a possible later addition, not needed now |
| Script output capture   | 10 MiB default per stream counted in raw bytes (previously UTF-16 units); incomplete trailing UTF-8 becomes one U+FFFD at end of stream, including after timeout or cancellation; a character split by the cap is dropped                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | WP-D3 (#232)                                                                                                                              | Existing output contract kept; raw bytes are the only honest unit for a byte cap                                                                                                                                | Reported `stdoutBytes` becomes a raw byte count                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Node baseline           | `^22` via pnpm `devEngines.runtime` (`onFail: error`), `.nvmrc` 22                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | WP-A1 (#263)                                                                                                                              | D-014                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Every pnpm command refuses Node 23; local checks on 22.14 sit below some dependencies' declared patch floors (ADR-0040)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Webhook return delivery | Five attempts in total including interrupted ones (restart never resets the budget); 10 s per-attempt deadline including the full response body; fixed delays of 2, 5, 10 and 20 s after failures one to four, no jitter, about 87 s worst case; `Idempotency-Key` header carrying one ULID per distinct channel allocated with `run.finished` (migrated identities `<run>:return:<seq>` and `<run>:webhook:N`); retried on connection refusal, timeout, HTTP 408, 429, 5xx and unknown transport outcomes; permanent on other 4xx, 3xx (redirects never followed) and invalid, non-HTTP(S) or credentialed destinations; positive acknowledgement is a complete 2xx bounded to 64 KiB; stop per D-021; projection indexed by `(run_id, type, seq)` with one query per page; migration 0015 freezes a budget of five for historical imports | WP-D2 (#246)                                                                                                                              | Reasonable defaults for a local single-process product (Opus review judgement); one policy source in contracts used by the engine and the HTTP adapter                                                          | Known gaps recorded as later bolts: no manual redelivery after a final failure, `Retry-After` on 429 ignored, no concurrency cap when recovery restarts many pending deliveries                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Subprocess test budgets | 60 s for API subprocess cases (children take 2.5 to 5 s quiet); V8 coverage of the child opt-in                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | WP-A5a (#317)                                                                                                                             | 20 s failed under machine load                                                                                                                                                                                  | Native shutdown evidence runs inside `test:coverage`; the api test tasks depend on the api build through turbo                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Inspector value bounds  | 4,096 characters laid out at once for a result, variable, message or thread JSON; 1,024 in a patch-diff column, a Node progress row or an Events payload cell; Show all renders the whole text in a scrolling region and Download JSON saves the exact stored value; 32 reconstruction results cached per inspector while the log grows (#259)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | about a hundred lines of pretty JSON fits a screen and lays out without a long task; cells stay scannable; a few threads' worth of memory |

## 7. Review findings and dispositions

### Wave of 2026-10-23: merges and rounds (2026-10-25, afternoon)

- **#185 (PR #404)** signed off in its renewal round 2 at `53de3d22` (target `e83b5b56`; 156 public-interface assertions including #265 recovery and #343 Event admission). **Merged** as `d4ac9731` ("Part of #185"). Its deferred docs 09 note fixed on `main` (`c923b7d3`).
- **#241 (PR #351)**: round 1's three browser failures and the focused-test finding fixed; CI then exposed four more (a selector anchored on a comma; the #319 shutdown spec relied on its own config's service-worker block and assertion budget), fixed in the specs. Round 2 **signed off** at `99a00169` (421 selected, 420 passed and the paid-provider skip on the first attempt; a seeded UI regression fails the gate; `.only` rejected). **Merged** as `9a3eded1`; #241 closed. The Browser job now gates every pull request. Docs 10's stale status text corrected on `main` (`e115bde3`).
- **#268 (PR #406)**: Sol implemented ranks 1 and 3 (finalization in 256-event pages with yields; first-attempt visit counts kept per execution), rank 2 did not reproduce, rank 4 measured only; the orchestrator reviewed every append and read path. QA round 1 passed with a minor benchmark finding (fixed). Audit round 1 **signed off** (27 + 4 boundary controls per runtime, a real exit between finalization pages; largest finalization read at 1,000 passes 262 to 322 ms → 7 to 9.5 ms; engine CPU -28 % and -40 %). **Merged** as `018de00f`; #268 closed. Deferred findings: stale evidence indexes (corrected on `main`, `aecc8eff`) and a port-allocation race in the API test helper (filed as #407).
- **#186 round 2 (PR #405)**: QA round 3 found no product defect. After `main` with #241 was merged, `qa.spec.ts` was rewritten for the 80-row Timeline (durable counts through the API), and the Browser job surfaced three real issues that were fixed: #7's live dot, dropped by the Timeline rewrite; the Event sequence field below 44 px on a coarse pointer; and a test read that threw on the bounded value's preparing note. CI then passed every job. QA round 4 was stopped as superseded. QA round 5 found no product defect and the full Edge suite passing; its verdict failed only on the six D-015 native-process tests, accepted as before. Audit round 1 runs at `215e57a3`.
- **#186 round 2 (PR #405), audits:** round 1 at `215e57a3` required changes (an oversized event's row lost the pinned labels; historical-thread pages decoded events past the requested boundary; double-encoded CHANGELOG characters). Fixed by the orchestrator (`437803aa`). Round 2 required changes again: the round-1 label fix kept a shortened copy of the event, which still overflowed for many small values (a 100,000-integer input, 125 patch operations), and the run's checkpoint was decoded before its sequence was compared with the boundary. Fixed by the orchestrator (`dc521653`): `describeEvent` joins description parts (recorded text, node and route IDs, the exit prefix) and a marker keeps only those parts, text cut to the 4,096-character preview budget; `getThreadCheckpoint(runId, atOrBefore?)` leaves a later checkpoint undecoded. Each regression failed against the previous code. Round 3 **signed off** at `dc521653` (13,608 description comparisons over all 31 event types, 48 further boundary-damage checks, 23 Edge cases with zero retries). `main` had gained only this register since the target (D-031). **Merged** as `f8694eb1`; #186 closed.
- Process note: an owner-upgrade rehearsal used port 6150 inside a QA allocation and stopped QA round 5's gateway once; rehearsals now use port 6250 and verify that the listener serves their own copy.

### Wave of 2026-10-23: merges and rounds (2026-10-25)

- **#255** merged as `d33ac0e5` ("Part of #255", open for owner acceptance) on its round-2 sign-off; round 1's exact-download finding was disposed of as unreachable through GraphGoblin, and the auditor agreed after tracing every recording path.

### WP-E1 (#259) — Sol review round 9: changes required; fixed by Fable (2026-10-22, D-026)

Fixes (`0d4ddb48`): a blur that leaves the component clears ownership and the pending hand-off (a removed control fires no blur, so its hand-off stands); the thread viewer defers the selected sequence, keeping the previous thread's body mounted under a "Reconstructing the thread at event N…" note while the deferred render replays the new one; a test file holds React's deferred value back on demand to observe the pending window for both components; the inspector page tests await the thread after a selection change. The implementer's position, put to round 10: what remains is one deferred replay and one deferred key enumeration per pathological value, off the interaction path but on the main thread; moving them to a worker is proposed as a follow-up issue rather than part of this bolt. Round 9 also measured the session log's synchronous `JSON.stringify` persistence (`event-store.ts`) at 150 to 191 ms on live updates, outside this delta, recorded for the measured-hot-path work.

The QA also saw fourteen editor and Settings tests time out under full coverage: the load class the hygiene task on `main` addresses. Sol review round 5, then QA round 2, then the audit.

### WP-D1 (#321) — Fable review of the Sol implementation (`2c675ce2`; PR #377 head `0e6ab27e` after merging `wp-d1-320`): confirmed with one note (2026-10-22, D-026)

As ADR-0046 and #278 require: the script handler appends a durable `script.dispatched { nodeId, visitId, dispatchId }` before calling the runner (after the workspace and secrets are prepared, with a cancel check), and on every entry `scriptVisit` reads the run's events: a dispatch for this node with no later `node.finished` of the node and no later `run.failed` fails the run with the resumable `SCRIPT_OUTCOME_UNCERTAIN` and the dispatch's provenance, so recovery never reruns it; `run.failed` disposes the attempt without ending the visit (the visit is the first `node.started` since the node last finished), so only an explicit resume runs the script again, with a fresh dispatch id, and a crash during that dispatch is uncertain again. The process runner reports a typed state: `definitely-not-started` for a synchronous spawn error (the Windows `.cmd` `EINVAL`), an `error` before `spawn` or a request already aborted; `outcome-uncertain` for an error after `spawn` (the tree is killed); `completed` on close. The handler maps them to `SCRIPT_START_FAILED` (resumable) and `SCRIPT_OUTCOME_UNCERTAIN`, keeps `SCRIPT_EXIT_CODE` and `SCRIPT_TIMEOUT` for completed runs, and treats an unclassified runner rejection as uncertain rather than `INTERNAL_ERROR`. `RunFailure.script` is optional and additive; the MCP summary adds `script` and `resumable` for script failures only, and `resume_run` states the explicit-choice rule. In the web the consent dialog is the only resume path for a failed run (`RunControls` renders nothing for terminal statuses); Cancel dispatches nothing; Replay forks at the node; `useRunEvents` re-arms a closed terminal tail from the saved cursor when the run snapshot's `lastEventSeq` passes it, so the resumed run is followed without a reload. `run-manager.ts` is unchanged; no migration. Non-blocking note: the unclassified runner rejection is reported without its error text in `details`; to be folded into any QA fix round. Evidence curated from 78 to 26 referenced files; local paths stripped.

### WP-D1 (#320) — Fable review of the Sol completion (`05080df2`): confirmed (2026-10-22, D-026)

Design as the Opus plan proposed and ADR-0046 records: `RunAdmission.parentNodeId` names the parent's subloop node; `SqliteTriggerAdmission.create` writes the parent's `child_run.started` (next parent sequence, `lastEventSeq` updated) in the same transaction as the child's run row, thread and `run.queued`, refuses a missing parent, requires the link to exist when the same child is admitted again, and notifies both logs only after commit; the in-memory fake stages the same writes and the shared admission contract covers rollback, same-id admission, the parent sequence and the missing-parent refusal. The subloop handler no longer records the link itself; the `startChild` service appends the committed link to the in-memory log, and `childOfThisVisit` finds it after a crash before the park. Finish identity: the child wake carries `childFinishedSeq` (the child's terminal-event sequence), `finishWithChild` consumes an outcome only when the last wake names that sequence (otherwise it re-parks for a fresh wake) and reuses a `child_run.finished` already recorded for it; `childOutcome` reads status, outcome, result and thread from one child-log snapshot; `notifyParent` skips a notification whose child has no terminal event (an independent resume made it obsolete). Tests: engine `child-creation.test.ts` (7), infrastructure `child-admission-recovery.test.ts` (6, real SQLite file, admission interrupted before and after the call, a trigger refusing the link, reopen), API `child-creation-restart.test.ts` (real kills at three delays; 36 root runs, 72 nested visits, one child each), plus the contract additions. Docs/04, docs/05, CHANGELOG, ADR-0046 (status set to accepted at this review). Round gates: build, typecheck, lint, format, layers, deps, docs, process recovery; engine 543 passed (98.10 / 92.36), infrastructure 624 passed with the D-015 cases, API 702 passed; the one engine and one API failure passed alone. Orchestrator actions: 41 local user paths in the evidence replaced by placeholders before the commit; the Opus WIP and the Sol completion squashed into one commit; branch `wp-d1-320` and PR #375 opened (Closes #320); Sol QA launched; the same Sol thread continues with #321 on the branch, to be stacked on #375.

### WP-F1 (#179) — Fable confirmation of round 6 (`ee0f4f40`): confirmed (2026-10-21, D-026)

The audit's P1 (a manual parent start admitted an unready pinned child and executed a preceding mutation before the child failed) is closed by a two-phase admission in the engine: `startRun` accepts an optional `beforeAdmission(plan)` policy; the intent (the root version and every pinned subloop child version, collected while `pinSubloops` walks the definition) is prepared under the pin lock, the lock is released for the policy's slow IO, and the same intent is committed after every version is re-asserted (`RunStartPlan`; `start-admission.test.ts`). The API's manual start supplies `assertRunReadiness` (`run-readiness.ts`): breadth first over the plan's pinned versions, depth bounded by `subloopDepthLimit` and node overrides, cycle protected, collecting inference and LLM-decision harnesses, one probe per distinct harness through the request-owned readiness memo (D-017) with distinct harnesses probed concurrently, issues naming the child loop and node; the start answers 422 `HARNESS_NOT_READY` with nothing written. Scheduled, webhook, internal-child and resume starts keep their behaviour. Nineteen API tests and four engine tests cover the audit scenario, both unready, shared and distinct harnesses, published-over-draft and numbered pins, the publish race during a probe, depth limits and overrides, a cycle, an LLM decision and the draft-root snapshot; native fake-CLI evidence before (202, zero probes, durable mutation) and after (422, one probe, no run). docs/06, the README's step 10 and the CHANGELOG updated. The round's gates: typecheck, lint, format, layers, deps, docs and build pass; engine 540 passed (98.02 percent lines, 92.33 percent branches); API 727 passed with eight load-class failures that pass alone; web 1,426 passed with load-class worker failures; Edge 16 of 22 with only the known #205 cases and the two F2b-owned Claude cases failing. Committed and pushed to PR #370; Sol QA round 2 running; F2b and F4 restack on this head once QA returns.

Blocking: B1 U08 spies on `ports.secrets` while webhook intake resolves through a fresh `secretsFor()` instance, so the assertion can never fire; B2 docs/09 lost the whole PWA bullet; B3 local paths and worker-process notes in the QA record and docs/10; B4 the subprocess fixtures never delete their temp directories (5 to 11 MB each with V8 coverage always on). Non-blocking: 20 s subprocess budgets fail under load; production `main.ts` measured coverage fell to 73/53/42 because shutdown handlers now run only in the Linux child (recorded for #264); a vacuous V8 entry assertion; the startup-ordering case is unexecuted and its `exited` assertion always true; U07 races two managers (accepted as a labelled race); U10 near-trivial; fragile libsql mock path; the harness needs a fresh `pnpm build`, which contradicts the `AGENTS.md` no-build rule; scratch files in branch history (squash merge); early harness runs inherited the owner's `JEV_API_KEY` into three disposable temp stores under a constant test master key (rows deleted by the implementer; directories deleted by the orchestrator on 2026-10-19; the harness now filters the variable; the owner may rotate the key). Residual: the four Linux-only cases (SIGINT, SIGTERM, startup ordering, buildApp fault) must be seen executing in CI before native shutdown counts as proven. Disposition: B1 to B4 and the accepted non-blocking items (budgets 60 s, api test tasks depend on build via turbo with an `AGENTS.md` sentence, ordering assertion, drop the V8 assertion, collapse U10, fail-fast mock path) sent to the Sol thread (`wp-a5a-2.md`); #264 and the lint gap recorded in `FOLLOWUPS.md`.

### WP-D3 (#232, #117) — Opus review of `beefcae`: approve with one required fix

#232 satisfied: one `StringDecoder` per stream through `BoundedOutputCapture`; the base defect reproduced with real subprocesses at offsets 2, 4, 5, 7, 8 and 9 of `AéX🦎Z`; raw-byte caps 0 to 11 on both streams; real-process cancellation with incomplete UTF-8 pending passes outside the sandbox (the sandbox `taskkill` denial did not reproduce for the reviewer, consistent with D-015). #117: bound enforced before parsing with a fixed 64 KiB buffer; the no-length stream is cancelled on overflow and the socket released; poll warnings carry `HTTP_PROBE_BODY_OVERFLOW` and never start a run; the old `.slice(0, 65_536)` truncation paths removed. Blocking: a response with no body (304/204) and a `Content-Length` above the cap is refused as overflow because the length check precedes the `!reader` return (regression from base; reproduced). Non-blocking: heartbeat runs fail with `INTERNAL_ERROR` rather than the typed code the docs claim and the path is untested (`heartbeat.ts` is WP-C2's file); the compressed pre-check compares compressed bytes against the decompressed cap; `script.ts:82` `stdoutBytes` counts UTF-16 units; the CHANGELOG upgrade note lacks how to find affected loops and the script-probe alternative, and guide 04/troubleshooting do not mention the cap; the split tests rely on a 60 ms child delay; docs/04 wording; the 64 KiB literals in `poll.ts:252` and heartbeat do not use the new constant; an unneeded buffer copy. Engineering parameter confirmed: 65,536 bytes in `packages/engine/src/ports.ts` as the port contract; single-result HTTP polls and heartbeats whose JSON exceeds 64 KiB (a GitHub list page with 30 to 100 issues) now fail explicitly instead of parsing a sliced body; this impact is named in the PR. Disposition: the blocker and the accepted items (heartbeat typed mapping and dead-slice removal as an additive edit with a rebase note for C2, raw byte counts on `ScriptRunResult` consumed at `script.ts:82` with a rebase note for C4, encoding-aware pre-check, complete upgrade note and guide text, deterministic split assertion, single constant) sent to the Sol thread (`wp-d3-2.md`).

### WP-E1a (#190) — Opus review of `d5e0478`: approve with required fixes

Confirmed: disposable API per workload (temp store, port 0, fake harness, no real adapters spawned), timing through the public `Container`/`RunManager` with behaviour-preserving port decorators, caps respected (10,000 visits and passes), four meaningful tests without private access, the three #268 candidates verified against code and profiles (full-row `runs.ts` reads carrying the 1.3 MB `thread_snapshot`; `countEntries` over the whole log on every fresh entry; one transaction per append). Blocking: B1 the browser TTI proxy stops at the page shell (about 110 DOM nodes, 0 bytes session storage at the recorded TTI; history arrives seconds later), so the headline inspector numbers and the #259 rank-1 evidence describe an empty shell; B2 the ranking omits the largest measured stall, the unlimited `events.read` at finalisation (`run-manager.ts:1615`) whose duration equals the maximum event-loop delay in every case (up to 40.9 s), and event-loop delay is not tabled; B3 local paths and process narration in the report and 733 absolute paths in a 635 KB JSON. Non-blocking: replay samples cannot observe the replay (synchronous native read and write loop) so the buffering columns are post-replay; array-append display finding belongs to #255; readiness cold start forks the harness worker rather than `main.js`; fresh/aged confounded by load order; heap citations; synthetic burst qualification; timeline windowing owner (assigned to #186); nits; tooling is not linted by `pnpm lint`. Disposition: all blocking items and the accepted non-blocking items sent to the Sol thread (`wp-e1a-2.md`); #255 evidence and the tooling lint gap recorded in `FOLLOWUPS.md`; the reviewer's quiet-host command list is kept for the official rerun.

### WP-B1 (#223, #318) — Sol QA of `30da5d7`: fail, adjudicated

Independent QA drove the built API on port 5610 over a disposable file store with SQL fault injection (report `docs/qa/2026-10-18-wp-b1.md` in the `codex-qa-wp-b1` worktree, to be carried into the PR). #223 passed every criterion (boundary prompts 9,999/10,000/10,001, rendered oversize, bounded 190,039-character failure with a visible marker, atomic batch rejection, restart without re-execution). #318: healthy recovery beside six damaged runs, manual repair and deletion, cancel-requested parent with a damaged child, diagnostics paging and the separate-process test all passed. Three defects: D1 (major as stated) a run with damaged events or `thread_snapshot` returns a 200 snapshot while `/events` or `/thread` return the typed 500; D2 (minor) finalized terminal damage is listed by diagnostics but absent from the startup log; D3 (minor, adjacent) MCP `list_runs` drops the REST continuation when a page decodes to zero items, so an MCP caller cannot page past damaged rows. Infrastructure coverage failed 14 tests: 7 native-process (sandbox `taskkill`, D-015) and 7 `upgrade.test.ts` 20 s timeouts under load. Disposition: D1 and D2 are the designed contract (D-016) and are to be documented in docs/05 and guide 07; D3 is fixed in round 3 (`wp-b1-3.md`, same Sol thread) with the REST cursor carried through and a public-interface test; the sandbox and load failures are not defects. Round-3 delta gets a short Opus confirmation before PR #349 merges.

## 9. Pending owner decisions and acceptance

Product decisions that define correct behaviour stay here and go to the owner as concrete questions with evidence. The "owner product review requested" bullets below (2026-10-19 and 2026-10-20) recorded the superseded gate (D-024): those PRs no longer wait for owner acceptance; they wait for their audit-found defects to be fixed, re-reviewed, re-tested and signed off by the designated Codex session (section 4b). The isolated-instance recipes they point to remain valid QA material.

- **Licence allowlist mismatch (found by the WP-A4 review, 2026-10-18).** `tooling/license-allowlist.json` has allowed BlueOak-1.0.0, Python-2.0, MIT-0 and CC-BY-4.0 since the M0 bootstrap, but `docs/11` ("Decided") and `docs/research/licenses.md` list only MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD-2-Clause, BSD-3-Clause, ISC, 0BSD, Unlicense and CC0, and say an ADR is needed to add one. BlueOak-1.0.0 is required by production dependencies (glob, lru-cache, minimatch, minipass, path-scurry); the other three appear only in dev dependencies. All four are permissive. Decision needed: approve the four with a short ADR and licence-research lines, or direct removal of the dependencies that need them. The delivery keeps the documented decision as is until then; the gate stays green either way.
- **Haiku 5.5 on Claude Code 2.1.292 (#206, 2026-10-18).** The implementer admitted `claude-sonnet-5-5` and `claude-haiku-5-5` with all five efforts after real turns on the installed CLI 2.1.292 reported exactly those ids. Anthropic's Claude Code docs name 2.1.293 as the minimum for Haiku 5.5; on 2.1.292 the model works but the CLI logs an `unrecognized_model` notice. `claude-haiku-4-5` was not admitted because the CLI drops effort for it. Decision needed with the #206 product review: accept Haiku 5.5 on 2.1.292 with the guide note (implementer's choice), or require the CLI update first. No product code depends on the answer beyond the guide wording.
- **#266 owner product review requested (2026-10-19)** on the issue: PR #355, isolated-instance steps in `docs/qa/2026-10-19-wp-b3-reset/README.md` (port 5650, disposable directories, in-repo seed helper), the `master.key` retention choice named for the owner to overturn. Merge of PR #355 (which also closes #188) waits for acceptance.
- **#206 owner product review requested (2026-10-19)** on the issue: PR #348, isolated-instance steps on port 5606, two paid turns, plus the Haiku 5.5 CLI-version decision. Merge of PR #348 waits for acceptance.
- **Heartbeat signal identity (#257/#272, found by the WP-C2 review, 2026-10-19; not blocking).** As implemented, a heartbeat node's signal identity (`waitId`) changes on every beat: a signal sent with the previous beat's identity gets 409 `WAIT_MISMATCH`, a signal sent between parks gets 409, and a retried signal inside one park counts twice. This is documented (docs/04 and docs/07) and satisfies the acceptance criteria (signals from earlier visits are rejected), but it is stricter than they require and makes external senders re-read the identity every beat. Alternative: a visit-scoped identity for heartbeat signals (stable from node entry to `node.finished`), which needs the engine to record signals that arrive between beats as durable targeted events for the next beat. Orchestrator default: keep the per-beat identity as shipped; the owner can choose the visit-scoped form as a later bolt. Signal idempotency keys are a separate possible bolt.
- **Defaults asymmetry (#179, found by both WP-F1 reviews, 2026-10-19; not blocking).** In Settings, Claude model choices in Defaults are gated by readiness while Codex choices stay editable (`DefaultsSection.tsx:147`). The adapters' metadata paths differ, but authentication, quota and transport failures do not justify blocking only Claude authoring. Both reviewers recommend a later bolt making the two symmetric: editable catalog choices with readiness warnings, while validation and run start keep the authoritative checks. Shipped as is; the owner can order the symmetric form.
- **Loops page latest-run badge (#231/#236, WP-G2 Opus confirmation, 2026-10-19; not blocking).** The badge validates only `id`, `status` and `createdAt`, so a latest run whose other columns are damaged still shows its status badge while the Runs list skips it (the inspector reports `STORED_RUN_INVALID`, diagnostics lists the run). Alternative for the owner review: show "latest run unavailable" whenever the full row fails to decode.
- **New defects found by the WP-C3 and WP-F5 reviews (2026-10-19), outside every I01 to I07 bolt; the orchestrator files them as issues for triage:** lone-surrogate dedupe keys never match their stored form, so such a poll candidate starts a new run on every poll; `.cmd`/`.bat` script commands make the script runner reject with a raw `spawn EINVAL` (`INTERNAL_ERROR`) instead of `PROCESS_START_FAILED`; `adapter-codex` `structured.ts` uses `mkdtemp` and fails with `ENAMETOOLONG` under long temp roots; a cwd-related spawn `ENOENT` is reported as "Claude could not be started" (not installed); docs/06 and `adapter-codex/options.ts` promise that every behaviour-affecting Codex default is overridden while the machine's `[windows] sandbox` setting is inherited.
- **Filed 2026-10-20 as `backlog` issues for triage (from the bullet above):** #361 lone-surrogate dedupe keys start a run every poll; #362 `.cmd`/`.bat` script commands rejected with a raw `spawn EINVAL`; #363 `adapter-codex` `structured.ts` `mkdtemp` under long temp roots; #364 a cwd-caused spawn `ENOENT` reported as not installed; #365 Codex sandbox mode inherited despite the documented override promise.
- **Owner product reviews requested 2026-10-20:** #129 (PR #366, README on port 5641), #231/#236 (PR #367, recipe on port 5631) and #179 (PR #370, README on port 5624 with the in-repo fake CLIs); each merges only after acceptance.
- (Superseded 2026-10-21, D-024/D-025.) Owner product acceptance is no longer requested per bolt. Each review-ready PR instead gets a review-ready checkpoint (PR URL, full head SHA, target branch and its tip, dependency or restack context, disposition of every finding, CI links, independent QA evidence, limitations) on the PR and in #279 for the designated Codex session's sign-off; any package assessed as needing post-merge human QA records its rationale and checklist in section 4b and on the issue before the label is applied.
